- The tests and labels copy the conventions of the cycle branches of Section 7 (the product of $\mu + 1$ for PD, the product of $\mu_i \mu_j - 1$ for NS, the nontrivial multipliers being all but the one nearest 1). I applied them to the multipliers of Section 8m without a source that does so for delay equations. I read no source on how DDE-BIFTOOL labels these points.
- $\Phi(T)$ of Section 8m is a discretization with $d = n(\mathrm{nhist} + 1)$ eigenvalues, of which only a few approximate multipliers of the delay equation. The others are small. On the model of Section 8l, with `nhist` from 4 to 24, their moduli were below about $1.5\cdot10^{-3}$. That was measured on one model. It is not proven, and a model with a very short period against its delays may behave differently. The tests use all $d$ eigenvalues. A small eigenvalue changes the factor $\mu + 1$ of PD and the factors $\mu\mu' - 1$ of NS by a small amount and keeps its sign, so it adds no zero as long as it stays small. The code does not check that it does.
- Each trial point of the locator builds $\Phi(T)$ again. I have not timed a long run.
- The closed forms of tests 1 and 2 are my own constructions (a scalar delay equation, and a block in a frame that turns with the orbit, below). I checked each with a numerical calculation of my own that is not kept in the repository. I did not take them from a source.
- LPC specials now appear on every run with `branch`, with or without `floquet`. The codim-2 tests of `run_branch` are not switched by `detect_*`, so no option turns the LPC label off. Section 8n had no specials. The points of a run do not change.
- The multipliers and the tests belong to the discretized problem on a fixed mesh (Section 8n). A mesh that is too coarse for the orbit moves the labels. Section 8p adds an opt-in move of the mesh.
- The tolerance tables are measured, not derived.
- The product for NS is formed with an exact power-of-two rescaling (by $2^{\pm 500}$, with the exponent tracked), so that a product that overflows in the plain loop saturates to a signed infinity instead of giving NaN. The result has the bits of the plain loop whenever no value in the plain loop overflows or falls into the subnormal range. Where the plain loop underflows, the rescaled product keeps digits the plain loop loses. The locator rule that skips an infinite test value with a warning (Section 5, item 5) holds on every run kind, so it also applies to the ODE problems of Sections 6 and 7, but only where a test value is infinite, which a finite test never is. No run of the tests reaches an overflow, so the saturation is tested on its own and not on a branch.

**Formulation.**
- *The multipliers.* The state block of the problem is $\Phi(T)$ of Section 8m at the profile, the period and the parameter of the point, with `nhist` from the `floquet` options. The engine takes all $d$ eigenvalues of it and stores them with the point. `floquet_multipliers` and the branch use the same code to build $\Phi(T)$, so they give the same eigenvalues for the same point.
- *The tests.* There are three, in this order, with the labels `LPC`, `PD` and `NS`. LPC is the $\alpha$ component of the tangent of the branch. Let $\mu_1, \dots, \mu_{d-1}$ be all eigenvalues except the one nearest 1 (the trivial multiplier). PD is the real part of $\prod_i (\mu_i + 1)$ and NS is the real part of $\prod_{i<j} (\mu_i \mu_j - 1)$. Both products are real, since the eigenvalues of a real matrix come in conjugate pairs. A sign change of a test between two accepted points is located and refined as for any other test of the branch engine, with `max_locate` and `tol_refine`. The NS product also vanishes when two real multipliers have product 1, so a zero of NS is labeled only if the pair $(\mu_i, \mu_j)$ whose product is nearest 1 is a complex conjugate pair, as in Section 7. Any other zero is dropped.
- *Without `floquet`.* The tests are $[\mathrm{LPC}, \mathrm{NaN}, \mathrm{NaN}]$, so only LPC is labeled.
- *The points.* A point keeps its profile, period and $\alpha$. With `floquet` it also gets the leading `count` multipliers by modulus and `trivial_error` of Section 8m. The corrector does not use them, so they do not change the points.
- *The phase reference.* As in Section 8n, a trial point of the locator uses the phase reference of the previous accepted point.
- *Cancellation.* The state block has no cancel flag, so $\Phi(T)$ is built without one. A flag set during a run stops it at the next step, as in Section 8n.

**The API.** In `pc_engine::dde`, re-exported with the `dde_` prefix:
- `continue_cycle_floquet(model, setup, start, opts, floquet, cancel) -> Result<DdeCycleBranch, EngineError>`, with `floquet: Option<&DdeFloquetOptions>` of Section 8m. `continue_cycle` of Section 8n is the call with `None`.
- `DdeCyclePoint` gets `multipliers: Vec<(f64, f64)>`, empty without `floquet`, and `trivial_error: f64`, NaN without `floquet`.
- `DdeCycleBranch` gets `specials: Vec<DdeCycleSpecial>` in the order of the branch, with `DdeCycleSpecial { label: String, index: usize, point: DdeCyclePoint }`. `index` is the index of the first point at or after the special point, as for `SpecialPoint` of `cont`. The `h` of `point` is the part of the step up to the special point, not the whole step, and its `newton_iters` are those of the trial or refinement that found it.
- The checks are those of Section 8n in the same order. With `floquet` they are followed by the checks of Section 8m on the options: the model has a delay, `nhist` $\ge 1$, the stage system $m\,d$ is at most 1000 and $1 \le$ `count` $\le d$. All of them are `BadProblem` and come before the start is corrected. The message of a check on the options starts with `floquet: `, so that a caller can tell it from the other `BadProblem`s.
- `check_cycle_floquet(model, setup, floquet) -> Result<(), EngineError>` runs the checks on the options alone. The command line calls it before it corrects a `hopf` start, so a bad `floquet` value is exit 2 and not a failed correction (exit 1) when the start does not converge.

**Command line.** `pc-cli dde-cycle FILE` with `branch: true` accepts the key `floquet` of Section 8m. The output of Section 8n gets these additions:
- With `floquet`, each point has `multipliers`, an array of `[re, im]` pairs, and `trivial_error`.
- The key `specials` is always present on a run with `branch`. It is an array in branch order, empty if there is no special point. Each element has `label`, `index`, `alpha`, `period` and `profile`, and with `floquet` also `multipliers` and `trivial_error`.
- Without `branch` nothing changes. `options.detect` and `options.mesh_adapt` with `branch` are still exit 2. A bad `floquet` value is exit 2 naming `floquet`, now also with `branch`.

**Acceptance tests.** Rust tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_labels.rs`, $N = 20$, $m = 4$ unless a test says otherwise, with the `nhist` and `count` of each test. Every model has the cycle $x = \sin t$, $T = 2\pi$ for every value of the parameter that is continued. The $x$ equation does not depend on the other states, and the other states are zero on the cycle. Tests 1 and 2 use the equation of Section 8l, `x' = -delay(x,tau)+mu*x*(1-x^2-delay(x,tau)^2)`, with $\mu = 0.3$ and $\tau = \pi/2$. The start is the exact orbit, corrected at its own parameter by the code of Section 8n.
1. NS, closed form. A second state $y' = -s\,y(t - 1)$ with `free = s`. Its multipliers on the cycle are $e^{2\pi\lambda}$ for the roots $\lambda$ of $\lambda + s\,e^{-\lambda} = 0$. For $0 < s < \pi/2$ all roots have negative real part. At $s = \pi/2$ the roots $\pm i\pi/2$ are on the imaginary axis, and for $0 < s < 2$ no other root is. The multipliers there are $e^{\pm i\pi^2}$, whose argument is $\pm\theta$ with $\theta = 4\pi - \pi^2 = 2.696766213269814$. The roots are real for $s < 1/e$ and form a double root $\lambda = -1$ at $s = 1/e$. Start at $s = 0.3$, `direction` $+1$, `bounds` $= (0.1, 2)$, `hmax` $= 0.2$, `floquet` with `nhist` $= 16$ and `count` $= 6$. The specials are exactly one `NS`. Its $\alpha$ equals $\pi/2$ within the bound of the table of test 1. Among the multipliers of that special point there is one within the bound of $e^{i\theta}$ and one within the bound of $e^{-i\theta}$. The double root at $s = 1/e$ gives no special.
2. PD, closed form. Two more states with $y' = s(-x_d y + x z) - \gamma y$ and $z' = s(x y + x_d z) - \gamma z$, $x_d = x(t - \tau)$, $\gamma = 0.3$ and `free = s`. On the cycle $x_d = -\cos t$, so the matrix of $(y, z)$ is $s\begin{pmatrix}\cos t & \sin t\\ \sin t & -\cos t\end{pmatrix} - \gamma I$. The first matrix is a reflection with eigenvalues $\pm 1$ whose eigenvectors turn by $t/2$. In the frame that turns with them the system is constant, with the matrix $\begin{pmatrix} s - \gamma & 1/2\\ -1/2 & -s - \gamma\end{pmatrix}$ and the eigenvalues $-\gamma \pm \sqrt{s^2 - 1/4}$. Over $T = 2\pi$ the frame turns by a half turn, which is $-I$, so the two multipliers are $-\exp\bigl(2\pi(-\gamma \pm \sqrt{s^2 - 1/4})\bigr)$. One of them equals $-1$ when $s^2 = 1/4 + \gamma^2$, that is $s = \sqrt{0.34} = 0.5830951894845301$. The pair is complex for $s < 1/2$, with modulus $e^{-2\pi\gamma}$, and real for $s > 1/2$. Start at $s = 0.4$, `direction` $+1$, `bounds` $= (0.3, 0.8)$, `hmax` $= 0.1$, `floquet` with `nhist` $= 16$ and `count` $= 6$. The specials are exactly one `PD`, with $\alpha = \sqrt{0.34}$ within the bound of the table of test 2, and one of its multipliers is within the bound of $-1$. A second run with `bounds` $= (0.3, 0.7)$ ends at $s = 0.7$, where the multiplier of largest modulus is $-\exp(2\pi(-\gamma + \sqrt{0.24}))$ within the bound. The transition at $s = 1/2$ gives no `NS` and no other special.
3. LPC, closed form. `x' = -delay(x,tau)+x*(a-(x^2+delay(x,tau)^2-1)^2)` at $\tau = \pi/2$. The orbit $x = r\sin t$ with $T = 2\pi$ is a solution when $a = (r^2 - 1)^2$, that is $r^2 = 1 \pm \sqrt a$, so $a = 0$ is a fold of the curve of cycles. Start at $a = 0.25$ on the upper branch ($r^2 = 1.5$), `free = a`, `direction` $-1$, `bounds` $= (-0.5, 0.4)$, `hmax` $= 0.1$. The run goes through the fold and comes back on the lower branch, and it stops with `Bounds` at $a = 0.4$. For a point with profile $u$, let $\rho^2$ be the mean over the mesh nodes $j$ of $u_j^2 + u_{j-N/4}^2$ (indices mod $N$), which equals $r^2$ for the exact orbit since $\tau/T = 1/4$. Before the fold $\rho^2 = 1 + \sqrt a$ and after it $\rho^2 = 1 - \sqrt a$, within the bound of the table of test 3. The specials that are labeled `LPC` are exactly one, with $\lvert\alpha\rvert$, $\lvert\rho^2 - 1\rvert$ and $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ within the bounds of the table. Without `floquet` the specials are that one `LPC` and nothing else. With `floquet` (`nhist` $= 12$, `count` $= 4$) the `LPC` special equals the one without `floquet` bit for bit in `alpha`, `period` and `profile`. The code does not assert that no `PD` or `NS` appears along this run, since I have not shown that.
4. The points do not change. For the runs of tests 1 to 3, the `alpha`, `period` and `profile` of every point with `floquet` equal those without, bit for bit, and the `LPC` specials (labels, `index`, `alpha`, `period`, `profile`) are the same.
5. The multipliers of the points. For the run of test 2, every point has exactly `count` multipliers, sorted by decreasing modulus. Each point's `multipliers` and `trivial_error` equal what `floquet_multipliers` of Section 8m returns for the profile, period and parameter of the point, bit for bit. Without `floquet`, `multipliers` is empty and `trivial_error` is NaN.
6. Errors. For the model of test 1, `floquet` with `nhist` $= 0$, with `count` $= 0$, with `count` $> d$ and with a stage system over 1000 is `BadProblem`. A `cancel` flag that is set at the call gives `Cancelled`. A bad step control together with a bad `floquet` gives the step-control message (the order of Section 8n).
7. The two evaluators. Tests 1 and 2 with `Evaluator::Jit` and `Evaluator::Fallback` give the same specials (labels and `index`), and their `alpha` agree within the bound of the table of test 7.
8. `pc-cli dde-cycle` with the problems of tests 1 to 3 and `branch: true` prints the `specials` of the engine (`label`, `index`, `alpha`, `period` and `profile` bit for bit, JIT). With `floquet` it also prints the `multipliers` and `trivial_error` of every point and special, bit for bit. Without `branch` the output of Section 8m is unchanged. `branch: true` with `floquet` is not exit 2 any more. `options.detect` and `options.mesh_adapt` with `branch` are still exit 2. A bad `floquet` value is exit 2 naming `floquet`, with `branch` and without, also with a `hopf` start and `options` of `max_newton` $= 1$, where the correction of the start would fail to converge and give exit 1 if the options were not checked first. The tests of the NS and PD problems (tests 1 and 2) run with `floquet`. The test of the LPC problem (test 3) runs with and without it.
9. Arithmetic and the locator. Unit tests in `cycle/codim2.rs` check that `pair_product` gives the bits of the plain loop on a range of inputs that neither overflow nor underflow, and that it saturates to a signed infinity, not NaN, on inputs whose plain product overflows. A unit test in `locate.rs` checks that an infinite test value at either end of a step, or at both, gives no special and exactly one warning, `ZZ is not finite between points 0 and 1; the test was skipped there` for the test model, and a second one checks that the name is the slot name when the label hook of the problem rejects every zero. That NaN gives no warning is checked by the existing test `nan_on_either_side_skips_the_test`.

**Measured tables.** The tables and bounds follow the first run of the tests (release build, $N = 20$, $m = 4$). Each bound is at most about 4 times the measured value (the ratios are 2.6 to 3.7) unless the table says otherwise.

| Test | Quantity | Measured | Bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | $\lvert\alpha - \pi/2\rvert$ at the `NS` special | $8.9\cdot10^{-16}$ | $10^{-12}$ |
| 1 | nearest multiplier to $e^{\pm i\theta}$ | $1.3\cdot10^{-9}$ | $4\cdot10^{-9}$ |
| 2 | $\lvert\alpha - \sqrt{0.34}\rvert$ at the `PD` special | $3.3\cdot10^{-12}$ | $10^{-11}$ |
| 2 | nearest multiplier to $-1$ at that point | $1.8\cdot10^{-15}$ | $10^{-12}$ |
| 2 | leading multiplier at $s = 0.7$ against the closed form | $1.2\cdot10^{-10}$ | $4\cdot10^{-10}$ |
| 3 | $\lvert\alpha\rvert$ at the `LPC` special | $5.8\cdot10^{-12}$ | $2\cdot10^{-11}$ |
| 3 | $\lvert\rho^2 - 1\rvert$ at that point | $3.8\cdot10^{-11}$ | $10^{-10}$ |
| 3 | $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ at that point | $2.3\cdot10^{-11}$ | $8\cdot10^{-11}$ |
| 3 | worst $\lvert(\rho - 1)^2 - \alpha\rvert$ over the run | $5.4\cdot10^{-10}$ | $2\cdot10^{-9}$ |
| 7 | $\alpha$ of a special, JIT against fallback | $0$ | $10^{-10}$ |

The first and fourth bounds are wider than 4 times the measured value, since those values are at rounding level and rounding differs between platforms. The last bound is wider since the measured difference is exactly zero. The runs gave `NS` at index 15 of 18 points, `LPC` at index 21 of 47 points, and 11 points for the `PD` run. The tests compare indices between runs (tests 4 and 7) and never against a fixed value.

**Reachability of one check.** `build_dde` refuses a model without a `delay(...)` call, so the check "the model has a delay" of the list under **The API** cannot fail for a model built through it. Test 6 reaches it by clearing the delay list of a built model, as the tests of Section 8m do. The check stays as a guard for a model made some other way.

## 8p. Mesh adaptation for cycles of a delay equation (phase E9b, step 4)

Step 4 of E9b lets the continuation of Sections 8n and 8o move the collocation mesh along the branch, so that an orbit that grows a steep part stays resolved. It is opt-in and off by default, and a run without it is the run of Sections 8n and 8o to the last bit. Section 8s names the slices of the MATLAB front end, and none covers this step yet.

**What is not verified.**
- The monitor is the one of the cycle branches of Section 7, the equidistribution of the jumps of the $m$-th derivative of the orbit (the AUTO-07p port in `cycle/mesh.rs`), applied to the profile alone. I read no source on how mesh points are chosen for a delay problem. The monitor does not look at the delayed arguments, so it measures the error of the profile and not the error that the delayed terms add.
- The collocation rows and the stage system of Section 8m on a mesh that is not uniform are my own. The only change is that the step of interval $j$ is $\delta_j$ and not $1/N$. Nothing is cited.
- The start mesh is always uniform. `correct_cycle`, `hopf_cycle`, `floquet_multipliers` and `monodromy` stay on the uniform mesh, and the code has no restart from an adapted mesh. The multipliers of a point of an adapted run exist only as the multipliers of the branch.
- The code does not estimate the discretization error along the branch. An orbit that grows a steep part faster than the mesh follows it is resolved only as well as the last adaptation allowed, and there is no warning.
- The locator and the labels compare test values across a mesh change, as the cycle branches of Section 7 do. Where the mesh moves between two points, a test value has a jump that is the difference of the two discretizations, and a label can move by that much.
- The orbit of the oracle model (below) is my own construction. A calculation of my own, not kept in the repository, gave the error of the uniform mesh at $G = 20$, $N = 20$, $m = 4$ (the baseline of table 1). The closed form of the Floquet multipliers of that model is also mine.
- On a smooth orbit the adapted mesh is less accurate than the uniform one. In table 3 the largest error is $2.2\times10^{-7}$ against $5.3\times10^{-8}$, about 4 times, and the mesh moves by up to 0.03 of the period. I have not found the cause. The tests bound this loss and do not remove it.
- A failed corrector adapts the mesh once per point even when `every` is `usize::MAX`, as `CycleProblem` of Section 7 does. Test 4 does not cover a run with a corrector failure, so the claim that a huge `every` gives the fixed-mesh run holds only for runs without one.
- The tolerance tables are measured from the code, not derived from the order of the method.
- The cost of the monitor is $O(N m n)$ per adaptation, which is small beside the dense Jacobian of Section 8l. The monodromy matrix is built on the current mesh as before. I have not timed a long run.

**Formulation.**
- *The mesh.* The mesh points are $0 = s_0 < s_1 < \dots < s_N = 1$ with $\delta_j = s_{j+1} - s_j$, in units of the period. On interval $j$ the $m + 1$ base points are $s_j + (l/m)\,\delta_j$ for $l = 0, \dots, m$ (the last one is the first of the next interval) and the Gauss points are $s_j + c_i\delta_j$. The unknowns are those of Section 8l and only their positions depend on the mesh.
- *The rows.* The collocation row at Gauss point $i$ of interval $j$ is $\delta_j^{-1}\,\dot p_j(\zeta_i) - T f$, where $p_j$ is the Lagrange polynomial of the interval in $\zeta \in [0, 1]$ and $f$ is evaluated at $u(s)$ and at the delayed values. With $\delta_j = 1/N$ this is the row of Section 8l. The periodicity rows do not change. The phase row does not change either, since the factors $\delta_j$ cancel in it, which is why it is mesh independent already in Section 8l.
- *The delayed points.* For delay $\tau_k$ the shifted point is $s' = \mathrm{frac}(s - \tau_k/T)$. It lies in the interval $j'$ with $s_{j'} \le s' < s_{j'+1}$, at $\zeta = (s' - s_{j'})/\delta_{j'}$, and a point that falls on a mesh node belongs to the interval that starts there, as in Section 8l. The derivatives with respect to $T$ and to a delay time are those of Section 8l with these $\delta$.
- *The monodromy matrix.* The stage steps of Section 8m are $h_j = T\delta_j$, the stage nodes are $s_j + c_i\delta_j$, and the delayed lookups are those above.
- *The uniform mesh.* While the mesh is the uniform one, the formulas of Sections 8l and 8m run as written there and give the same bits. Only after the first adaptation does the general form run.
- *The adaptation.* As `CycleProblem` of Section 7: every `every` accepted points, and after a failed corrector, at most once per accepted point, the new mesh comes from the profile and the current mesh (`new_mesh`, with the orbit periodic). The profile and the tangent are interpolated onto the new mesh, with $T$ and $\alpha$ unchanged. The interpolated profile becomes the phase reference. When every $m$-th derivative is below $10^{-7}$, or the interpolation cannot get its memory, the mesh stays.
- *The order in a step.* The specials of a step are located on the mesh of that step. Then the point is accepted, then the mesh adapts. A point stores the mesh its own profile lives on, and a special stores the mesh of the step that contains it.

**The API.** In `pc_engine::dde`, re-exported with the `dde_` prefix:
- `continue_cycle_adapt(model, setup, start, opts, floquet, adapt, cancel) -> Result<DdeCycleBranch, EngineError>`, with `floquet: Option<&DdeFloquetOptions>` as in Section 8o and `adapt: &DdeMeshAdapt`. `DdeMeshAdapt { every: usize }`, with `every` the number of accepted points between adaptations. `continue_cycle` and `continue_cycle_floquet` are unchanged and never adapt.
- `DdeCyclePoint` gets `mesh: Vec<f64>`, the $N + 1$ mesh points of the profile of that point, empty on the points of `continue_cycle` and `continue_cycle_floquet`. With `adapt` the first point has the uniform mesh $j/N$, computed as `j as f64 / N as f64`.
- The checks are those of Section 8o in the same order, followed by two checks on `adapt`: `every` $\ge 1$ and `ntst` $\ge 2$. Both are `BadProblem` with a message that starts with `adapt: `, and both come before the start is corrected.
- `opts.mesh_adapt` is still ignored by the three functions. Its default is 3 for the other problem types, so honoring it would change the results of Sections 8n and 8o. The only way to adapt is `DdeMeshAdapt`.

**Command line.** `pc-cli dde-cycle FILE` with `branch: true` accepts the key `adapt`, an object with one key, `every`, an integer of at least 1 that has no default. Without `adapt` the output is that of Sections 8n and 8o. With it, every point and every special has `mesh`, an array of $N + 1$ numbers. A bad `adapt` is exit 2 naming `adapt` (not an object, no `every`, `every` not an integer of at least 1, any other key, `ntst` = 1, and `adapt` without `branch`). It is checked before a `hopf` start is corrected, as `floquet` is. `options.mesh_adapt` is still exit 2, and its message names `adapt`.

**Acceptance tests.** Rust tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_adapt.rs`, unit tests in `crates/pc-engine/src/dde/cycle.rs` and `floquet.rs`, and CLI tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`. $N = 20$, $m = 4$ unless a test says otherwise.

The oracle model STEEP is, with $G = 20$,

    par c=0.5, tau=1
    x'=-x+c*(delay(x,tau)-tanh(G*delay(y1,tau)))+tanh(G*y1)+G*(1-tanh(G*y1)^2)*(-y2)
    y1'=y1-y2-y1*(y1^2+y2^2)
    y2'=y1+y2-y2*(y1^2+y2^2)

with the number 20 written out in the source. For every $c$ and $\tau$ it has the orbit $y_1 = \cos t$, $y_2 = \sin t$, $x = \tanh(G\cos t)$ with $T = 2\pi$, since then $x(t - \tau) = \tanh(G\,y_1(t - \tau))$ and the $c$ term vanishes, and $\dot x = -G\sin t\,(1 - \tanh^2(G\cos t))$, which is the rest of the right-hand side. The orbit has two steep parts, of width about $1/G$ in $t$, at $t = \pi/2$ and $3\pi/2$. The variational equation is block triangular. The $(y_1, y_2)$ block is that of the Stuart-Landau oscillator and has the multipliers $1$ and $e^{-4\pi}$. The $x$ block is $\xi' = -\xi + c\,\xi(t - \tau)$ with constant coefficients and has the multipliers $e^{2\pi\lambda}$ for the roots $\lambda$ of $\lambda + 1 = c\,e^{-\lambda\tau}$. None of this depends on $G$. For $c = 0.5$ and $\tau = 1$ the rightmost root is real, $\lambda_0 = -0.3152$ (to be found by Newton's method in the test), and the multiplier is $e^{2\pi\lambda_0} \approx 0.138$. The error of a profile is the largest absolute difference from the exact orbit over the base points, at the positions $s_q$ that the mesh of the point gives.

1. The mesh follows the steep parts. STEEP, `free = c`, the start is the exact orbit on the uniform mesh at $c = 0.5$ and is corrected by the code of Section 8n, `direction` $+1$, `bounds` $= (0.3, 0.7)$, `hmax` $= 0.1$, `max_steps` as in table 1, `adapt` with `every` $= 1$, no `floquet`. The first point has the uniform mesh and the error $e_0$ of the uniform mesh (the baseline of table 1). At every point the mesh has $N + 1$ values, starts at 0, ends at 1 and increases strictly. The error of the last point is at most the bound of table 1, which is a fraction of $e_0$, and $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ is at most the bound there. The largest $\lvert s_j - j/N\rvert$ of the last mesh is at least the bound there, so the mesh has moved, and its smallest interval lies in a steep part ($t$ within 0.3 of $\pi/2$ or $3\pi/2$).
2. The multipliers on an adapted mesh. The run of test 1 with `floquet` ($\mathrm{nhist} = 16$, $\mathrm{count} = 2$). At every point the leading multiplier is within the bound of table 2 of $1$ (`trivial_error` is at most the bound there) and the second one is within the bound there of $e^{2\pi\lambda_0}$. The `alpha`, `period` and `profile` of every point equal those of test 1 bit for bit, and so does every `mesh`.
3. A smooth orbit stays accurate. The $\mu$-line of Section 8n, test 1 ($K = 0$, `direction` $+1$, same options), with `adapt` and `every` $= 1$. At every point the mesh is valid, $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ and the error against $\sin 2\pi s_q$ are at most the numbers of table 3, and the largest $\lvert s_j - j/N\rvert$ over the run is at most the bound there. The adapted mesh is not better here: its largest error is about 4 times that of the uniform mesh (table 3), and the test only bounds the loss.
4. The uniform path. For the runs of Section 8n, test 1 ($K = 0$, both directions), Section 8o, test 2 and test 3, `continue_cycle_adapt` with `every` $=$ `usize::MAX` (so that no adaptation is due) gives the points and specials of `continue_cycle_floquet`, bit for bit, and every `mesh` is the uniform mesh. `continue_cycle` and `continue_cycle_floquet` give an empty `mesh` on every point and special.
5. The rows on a mesh. A unit test in `dde/cycle.rs` with a hand-made non-uniform mesh (the intervals alternate between $0.5/N$ and $1.5/N$, and $N = 20$): the Jacobian of the rows agrees with central differences to a relative $10^{-6}$ for the STEEP model at a perturbed exact orbit, for the columns of the profile, $T$ and $\alpha$, with `free = tau` and with `free = c`. On the uniform mesh given explicitly as $j/N$, the residual and the Jacobian agree with those of the uniform path to $10^{-12}$ relative to $1 + \lvert\cdot\rvert$. For the OSC model of Section 8l ($K = 0$), the exact orbit sampled on the hand-made mesh has a residual at most the bound of table 4. A shifted point that falls exactly on a mesh node is assigned to the interval that starts there.
6. The monodromy on a mesh. A unit test in `dde/floquet.rs`: with the uniform mesh given explicitly, `monodromy` and the version with a mesh agree to $10^{-10}$ in the eigenvalues. On the hand-made mesh of test 5, the OSC exact orbit and `nhist` $= 16$, the leading eigenvalues agree with those of `floquet_multipliers` to the bound of table 4.
7. Errors. For the STEEP model, `adapt` with `every` $= 0$ and with `ntst` $= 1$ is `BadProblem` with a message that starts with `adapt: `. A bad step control together with a bad `adapt` gives the step-control message, and a bad `floquet` together with a bad `adapt` gives the message that starts with `floquet: `. A `cancel` flag that is set at the call gives `Cancelled`.
8. The two evaluators. Test 1 with `Evaluator::Jit` and `Evaluator::Fallback` gives the same number of points, and `alpha`, `period`, `profile` and `mesh` agree to $10^{-9}$ relative to $1 + \lvert\cdot\rvert$.
9. `pc-cli dde-cycle` with the problem of test 1 and `branch: true`, `adapt: {"every": 1}` prints the points of `continue_cycle_adapt` (the `alpha`, `period`, `profile` and `mesh` of every point and special bit for bit, JIT). With `floquet` it also prints the multipliers. Without `adapt` the output has no `mesh` key and equals the output of Sections 8n and 8o. The exit-2 cases of the command line paragraph each give exit 2 with the right name in the message, with a Hopf start as well, and the message of `options.mesh_adapt` names `adapt`.

**Measured tables.** The values are from `cargo test --release -p pc-engine --test dde_cycle_adapt -- --ignored --nocapture` (the ignored test `print_tables`), run on the development machine. Each bound is about 4 times the measured value, in the safe direction, except where the row says otherwise.

Table 1, STEEP, `every` $= 1$, `max_steps` $= 60$ (10 points, the run stops on the bounds of $c$):

| Quantity | Measured | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| error $e_0$ of point 0 (uniform mesh) | $7.833\times10^{-2}$ | none, it is the baseline |
| error of the last point | $8.547\times10^{-3}$ ($0.109\,e_0$) | at most $0.4\,e_0$ |
| largest $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ over the run | $2.450\times10^{-8}$ | at most $10^{-7}$ |
| largest $\lvert s_j - j/N\rvert$ of the last mesh | $1.184\times10^{-1}$ | at least $0.03$ |
| position of the smallest interval of the last mesh | $t = 1.530$ | within 0.3 of $\pi/2$ or $3\pi/2$ |

The error of the points, in order, is $7.8\times10^{-2}$ for points 0 and 1 (point 0 is the start, and the corrector builds and stores point 1 before the first adaptation, so both are on the uniform mesh), then $1.2\times10^{-2}$, $1.3\times10^{-2}$, $9.7\times10^{-3}$, $9.7\times10^{-3}$, $8.6\times10^{-3}$ and $8.5\times10^{-3}$ for the rest. The adapted mesh lowers the error by a factor of about 9 on this orbit. I have not looked into why it stops there.

Table 2, the same run with `floquet` ($\mathrm{nhist} = 16$, $\mathrm{count} = 2$):

| Quantity | Measured | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| `trivial_error`, every point | $5.3\times10^{-15}$ to $1.5\times10^{-14}$ | at most $10^{-11}$, the bound of Section 8o, not 4 times the value |
| distance of the second multiplier from $e^{2\pi\lambda_0}$ | $1.0\times10^{-12}$ at point 0, rising to $1.43\times10^{-9}$ at point 9 | at most $6\times10^{-9}$ |

Table 3, the $\mu$-line (OSC, $K = 0$), `every` $= 1$ (13 points):

| Quantity | Measured | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| largest $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert$ | $1.698\times10^{-8}$ | at most $7\times10^{-8}$ |
| largest error against $\sin 2\pi s_q$, adapted mesh | $2.239\times10^{-7}$ | at most $9\times10^{-7}$ |
| largest error, uniform mesh (for comparison, no bound) | $5.297\times10^{-8}$ | none |
| largest $\lvert s_j - j/N\rvert$ over the run | $2.897\times10^{-2}$ | at most $0.12$ |

Table 4, the hand-made mesh of test 5 (intervals alternate between $0.5/N$ and $1.5/N$), OSC, release build:

| Quantity | Measured | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| largest collocation residual of the exact orbit | $3.44\times10^{-5}$ | at most $1.5\times10^{-4}$ |
| largest distance of the two leading multipliers from those of the uniform mesh (`nhist` $= 16$) | $2.74\times10^{-8}$ | at most $1.2\times10^{-7}$ |

**One-time check of the uniform path.** Before the code of this step was written, the output of `pc-cli dde-cycle` for three problems (the $\mu$-line with `branch`, the same with `floquet`, and the STEEP model with `branch` and `floquet`, all without `adapt`) was saved. After the step the same three problems give the same output byte for byte. This was run once on the development machine, with the debug build, and is not a test, because the last bits of `sin` and `tanh` differ between platforms.

## 8q. Branch points of cycles of a delay equation (phase E9b, step 5)

Step 5 of E9b labels the branch points of the discretized cycle problem, with the label `BPC`, along the continuation of Sections 8n to 8p. It is opt-in and off by default, and a run without it is the run of those sections to the last bit. It does not switch branches (Section 8r does). Section 8s names the slices of the MATLAB front end, and none covers this step yet.

**What is not verified.**
- The test function is my own choice and has no source. It follows the pattern of $\psi_H$ in Section 5, a sign times a bounded magnitude, and the reason for it is below. I did not read how DDE-BIFTOOL or AUTO-07p detect branch points of cycles.
- The claim that the determinant of the bordered matrix changes sign at a branch point and not at a fold is the argument of the BP test of Section 5, carried over to the matrix of Section 8l. I did not prove it for the delay problem. The tests check it on models with a known branch point and on models with a fold, a PD point and an NS point.
- A point where an even number of branches cross has no sign change of the determinant and is missed. Test 8 pins one such case (two identical decoupled blocks), so that a later change that finds it has to change this section.
- The branch is that of the discretized problem. The oracle (below) rests on the claim that the collocation matrix of the scalar linear periodic problem $\zeta' = aT\zeta$ is singular only at $a = 0$. That holds for the stability function of Gauss collocation, a diagonal Padé function, at the real values I looked at, and I did not prove it for every $m$. The tests use $m = 4$ and $m = 6$ and count the labels.
- The tolerances are measured on decoupled-block models only. A model whose branch point comes from a coupled mode may locate less exactly, and nothing here measures that.
- With `adapt` (Section 8p) the locator compares test values across a mesh change, so the location is less exact (test 7). The test value is not continuous across a mesh change, and the label can move by the difference of the two discretizations.
- Branch switching at a `BPC` is not written in this step. The run goes on along the branch it was on, and the label only reports the point. Switching is Section 8r.
- A `BPC` is not checked against a second multiplier at $+1$. With `floquet` the multipliers at the point are in the output, and the code does not compare them with the label.
- Delay cycles only. The ODE cycles of Section 7 have no `BPC`, and `detect_bp` has no effect on them.
- The corrector near a branch point was measured on the same models only (it converged at every point there, with no warning).

**The test function.** At a point $k$ the bordered matrix $B_k$ is the one whose solve gives the tangent in `run_branch`: the $\dim - 1$ rows of the Jacobian of Section 8l and a last row equal to the previous tangent $v_{k-1}$. Section 5 takes $\det B_k$ as $\psi_{BP}$. For a cycle the determinant overflows and underflows, since $\dim$ runs to 3000, and `Lu::det` saturates at $\pm$`f64::MAX` or $\pm$`f64::MIN_POSITIVE`. The sign test of the locator, which multiplies two stored values, then fails. So the engine stores

$$\psi_{BPC} = \operatorname{sign}(\det B_k)\,\min_i \lvert u_{ii}\rvert,$$

with $u_{ii}$ the pivots of the LU factorization of $B_k$. It has the sign of the determinant and is 0 exactly where a pivot is 0, which is where the determinant is 0. Its modulus is at most the largest entry of $B_k$, so it does not overflow, and it underflows only at a zero. Where $B_k$ is singular the engine does what Section 5 does for BP, a determinant of 0 and the kernel tangent. A step that ends exactly on a branch point (a predictor that already solves the system on a singular matrix) is accepted as a branch point met exactly, as in Section 5.

**The label.** A sign change of $\psi_{BPC}$ between two accepted points is located by the search of Section 5 (Illinois regula falsi, trial points corrected on the hyperplane of the step) and reported as a special of `DdeCycleBranch` with `label` `"BPC"`, in order with the `LPC`, `PD` and `NS` specials. The first point of a run follows the first-point rule of Section 5 for the BP test. The sign of $\det B_k$ flips with the sign of the tangent at the first point, as it does for BP, and the first step is handled as in Section 5.

**The hook.** `Problem` gets `bp_label(&self) -> Option<&'static str>`, whose default is `Some("BP")` where `ep_tests()` is true and `None` otherwise. The detection code (`tests_fn::evaluate`, `locate`) stores $\psi_{BP}$ in the BP slot when `detect_bp` is on and `bp_label` is `Some`, and names the label and the warning slot with it. Every problem that exists today keeps its label and its slot values. The cycle problem of a delay equation returns `Some("BPC")` when the run asks for it and `None` otherwise, and its `bordered_solve` returns $\psi_{BPC}$ in place of the determinant in that case. The corrector never reads the determinant, so the points of a run do not change. `Lu` gets `min_pivot(&self) -> f64`, the smallest $\lvert u_{ii}\rvert$ (`INFINITY` for $n = 0$). The default `bordered_solve` and the one of the cycle problem build the matrix in one shared helper, so the two cannot differ.

**The API.** In `pc_engine::dde`, re-exported with the `dde_` prefix: `continue_cycle_bpc(model, setup, start, opts, floquet, adapt, cancel) -> Result<DdeCycleBranch, EngineError>`, with `floquet: Option<&DdeFloquetOptions>` and `adapt: Option<&DdeMeshAdapt>`. It is `continue_cycle_floquet` (with `adapt` `None`) or `continue_cycle_adapt` (with `Some`) with the BPC test on. The checks are those of Sections 8o and 8p in the same order. `continue_cycle`, `continue_cycle_floquet` and `continue_cycle_adapt` are unchanged and never label `BPC`. `opts.detect_bp` is still ignored by all of them, so the only way to get a `BPC` is this function. No C function and no MATLAB function changes.

**Command line.** `pc-cli dde-cycle FILE` with `branch: true` accepts the key `bpc`, a boolean that is off when it is absent or `false`. With `bpc: true` the output has the specials of `continue_cycle_bpc`, with `floquet` and `adapt` as before. A `bpc` that is not a boolean, and `bpc: true` without `branch`, are exit 2 naming `bpc`. `options.detect_bp` is still ignored. Without `bpc` the output is that of Sections 8n to 8p.

**Acceptance tests.** Rust tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_bpc.rs`, unit tests in `crates/pc-linalg/src/dense.rs`, `crates/pc-engine/src/problem.rs` and `crates/pc-engine/src/dde/cycle.rs`, and CLI tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`. $N = 20$, $m = 4$ and `Evaluator::Jit` unless a test says otherwise. The runs use `hmax` $= 0.1$ and `max_steps` $= 400$ unless a test says otherwise.

The oracle model ZBLK is, with $\pi/2$ written out as `tau`,

    par mu=0.1, tau=1.5707963267948966, m0=0.3
    x'=-delay(x,tau)+mu*x*(1-x^2-delay(x,tau)^2)
    z'=(mu-m0)*z-z^3

For every $\mu$ it has the orbit $x = \sin t$, $z = 0$ with $T = 2\pi$, since $x(t - \tau) = \sin(t - \pi/2) = -\cos t$ and then $\dot x = -x(t-\tau) + 0 = \cos t$. The $z$ equation is decoupled and has the equilibrium $z = 0$, so the variational equation is block diagonal. Its $z$ block is $\zeta' = (\mu - m_0)\zeta$, a scalar linear periodic problem, and its discretization is singular exactly at $\mu = m_0$. So the discretized problem has a branch point on the line at $\mu = m_0$, where the nontrivial branch $z^2 = \mu - m_0$ crosses, and the Jacobian is block diagonal for the same reason. The start is the exact orbit, `free = mu`, `bounds` $= (0.1, 0.6)$, `direction` $+1$ unless a test says otherwise.

1. The exact branch point. ZBLK, start $\mu = 0.1$, no `floquet`, no `adapt`. The run has exactly one special and its label is `BPC`, with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-13}$. The same run from $\mu = 0.6$ with `direction` $-1$ has the same. `continue_cycle_floquet` on the same inputs has no special with the label `BPC`, and neither has `continue_cycle_adapt`.
2. Step size and start. ZBLK from the off-grid start $\mu = 0.137$ with $h_{\max} = 0.083$ and with $h_{\max} = 0.31$: exactly one special, `BPC`, with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-13}$.
3. Floquet and size. ZBLK with `floquet` ($\mathrm{nhist} = 20$, $\mathrm{count} = 4$), and ZBLK with $N = 100$, $m = 4$ and no `floquet`: in each, exactly one special, `BPC`, with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-13}$.
4. A model with a nonlinear coefficient. The `z` equation replaced by `z'=(exp(8*mu)-exp(8*m0))*z-z^3`, start $\mu = 0.137$, $h_{\max} = 0.31$: exactly one `BPC` with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-13}$.
5. Branch point, fold, branch point. The model

        par a=0.25, tau=1.5707963267948966, m0=0.2
        x'=-delay(x,tau)+x*(a-(x^2+delay(x,tau)^2-1)^2)
        z'=(a-m0)*z-z^3

   with `free = a`, the start $x = \sqrt{1.5}\,\sin 2\pi s_q$, $z = 0$, $T = 2\pi$, `bounds` $= (-0.5, 0.4)$, `direction` $-1$, `floquet` as in test 3. The specials in order of $\alpha$ along the run are `BPC`, `LPC`, `BPC`. Both `BPC` have $\lvert a - 0.2\rvert \le 10^{-13}$. The `LPC` is at the fold of the cubic branch, which is $a = 0$ (Section 8o, test 3), with $\lvert a\rvert \le 2\times10^{-11}$, the bound of that test.
6. No false label. Each of these runs, with `floquet` where the model has the labels of Section 8o, has no `BPC`: ZBLK with $m_0 = 1.0$ (no branch point in the range); the OSC model of Section 8l alone on the $\mu$-line of Section 8n, test 1 ($K = 0$); the cubic model of test 5 without the $z$ equation (it has the `LPC`, and the test checks that the fold gives no `BPC`); and the model

        par mu=0.3, tau=1.5707963267948966, s=0.3
        x'=-delay(x,tau)+mu*x*(1-x^2-delay(x,tau)^2)
        y'=-s*delay(y,1)

   with `free = s`, `bounds` $= (0.1, 2.0)$, $h_{\max} = 0.2$, which has an `NS` point and no `BPC`. The test also checks that the `NS` is still labeled.
7. Mesh adaptation. ZBLK with `adapt` ($\mathrm{every} = 3$), no `floquet`: exactly one special, `BPC`, with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 3\times10^{-10}$ and $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert \le 5\times10^{-8}$ at it. The meshes of the points are valid as in Section 8p.
8. The limitation. ZBLK with a second block `w'=(mu-m0)*w-w^3` and the start with $z = w = 0$: the run has no `BPC`, since the determinant does not change sign at a double branch point. This test pins the limitation of the third item of the list above. If it fails, the label found something that the section says it cannot, and the section changes with the code.
9. The points do not change. For the $\mu$-line of Section 8n (test 1, $K = 0$, both directions) and the runs of Section 8o, tests 1 to 3, `continue_cycle_bpc` gives the same `alpha`, `period` and `profile` at every point, and the same `LPC`, `PD` and `NS` specials, as `continue_cycle_floquet`, bit for bit. The same holds with `adapt` against `continue_cycle_adapt` on the $\mu$-line of Section 8p, test 3. Where a run has no `BPC` (all of these), the two outputs are equal as values.
10. The pieces. Unit tests: `Lu::min_pivot` is 1 for the identity, 0.5 for $\mathrm{diag}(2, 0.5, 3)$ and `INFINITY` for $n = 0$, and for $\mathrm{diag}(10^{200}, 10^{200}, 10^{200})$ and $\mathrm{diag}(10^{-200}, 10^{-200}, 10^{-200})$ the determinant saturates while `min_pivot` is exactly $10^{200}$ and $10^{-200}$; `bp_label` is `Some("BP")` for an equilibrium problem and `None` for each curve problem that has `ep_tests()` false; an equilibrium run through a branch point still gives the label `BP`; the cycle problem has `bp_label` `None` by default and `Some("BPC")` after the run asks for it; and the bordered solve of the cycle problem without the BPC test equals the default solve of `Problem` in the solution and in the determinant, bit for bit, on the matrix of ZBLK at its start.
11. Errors. `continue_cycle_bpc` for ZBLK: `adapt` with $\mathrm{every} = 0$ and with `ntst` $= 1$ are `BadProblem` with a message that starts with `adapt: `, a bad `floquet` is `BadProblem` with `floquet: `, a bad step control gives the step-control message first, and a `cancel` flag that is set at the call is `Cancelled`.
12. The two evaluators. Test 1 with `Evaluator::Jit` and `Evaluator::Fallback` gives the same number of points and specials, and the `alpha` of the `BPC` agrees to $10^{-9}$. The `BPC` of the fallback run is also held to the bound of test 1.
13. `pc-cli dde-cycle` with ZBLK as the problem, `branch: true` and `bpc: true` prints one special with the label `BPC` and $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-11}$ in the JSON, and the points are those of the same run without `bpc` (the `alpha`, `period` and `profile` of every point bit for bit). It also works with `floquet`, with `adapt`, and with both. Without `bpc`, and with `bpc: false`, the output has no `BPC` and equals the output of Sections 8n to 8p. `bpc: 1`, `bpc: "yes"`, and `bpc: true` without `branch` are exit 2 naming `bpc`, with a Hopf start as well.

**Measured values.** The first bounds came from a feasibility run before the code of this step was written, on the development machine, with a prototype of the hook in a scratch copy of the engine (not committed). On the exact oracles with a uniform mesh the error of $\alpha$ was at the rounding level, about $10^{-15}$, with and without `floquet`, at $N = 100$, with a nonlinear block, with the off-grid start and with the step $h_{\max} = 0.31$. With `adapt` the error was $7.4\times10^{-11}$ in $\alpha$ and $1.2\times10^{-8}$ in $T - 2\pi$. The sequence `BPC`, `LPC`, `BPC` was found. No false label appeared at the `NS` point or at the fold, and the double branch point was missed.

The final tests print what they measure with `cargo test -p pc-engine --test dde_cycle_bpc -- --ignored --nocapture` (the test `print_tables`). On the development machine, in the `dev` profile:

| Run | Point | $\alpha - m_0$ | $T - 2\pi$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Test 1, direction $+1$ | 8 | $-1.6\times10^{-15}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 1, direction $-1$ | 9 | $-1.6\times10^{-15}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 2, $h_{\max} = 0.083$ | 7 | $-5.6\times10^{-17}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 2, $h_{\max} = 0.31$ | 7 | $-5.6\times10^{-17}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 3, `floquet` | 8 | $-1.6\times10^{-15}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 3, $N = 100$ | 8 | $-6.6\times10^{-15}$ | $0$ |
| Test 4, nonlinear block | 7 | $5.6\times10^{-17}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 12, fallback evaluator | 8 | $-1.6\times10^{-15}$ | $1.9\times10^{-11}$ |
| Test 7, `adapt` | 8 | $-7.4\times10^{-11}$ | $-1.2\times10^{-8}$ |

Test 5 gives `BPC` at point 7 ($a - 0.2 = -1.3\times10^{-15}$), `LPC` at point 21 ($a = 5.8\times10^{-12}$) and `BPC` at point 37 ($a - 0.2 = 2.1\times10^{-15}$).

The bounds of tests 1 to 5 and 12 without `adapt` are $10^{-13}$, 15 to 50 times the largest value above, so that another platform's libm does not trip them. The bounds of test 7 are about 4 times the table. The `LPC` bound of test 5 is the one of Section 8o, test 3, about 3.4 times the value above. The agreement bound of test 12 ($10^{-9}$) and the CLI bounds of test 13 ($10^{-11}$, and $3\times10^{-10}$ with `adapt`) are looser than the values and were not set from a measurement. The table comes from one machine and one build. The tests with the $10^{-13}$ bounds also pass in the `macos-jit` CI job on the commit that holds the table, but the values measured there are not recorded.

## 8r. Branch switching at a branch point of cycles of a delay equation (phase E9b, step 6)

Step 6 of E9b leaves a `BPC` of Section 8q on the other branch of the discretized cycle problem and continues along it. It is Rust only. No C function, no MATLAB function and no dependency changes. The entry points are new, and `continue_cycle`, `continue_cycle_floquet`, `continue_cycle_adapt` and `continue_cycle_bpc` are unchanged.

**What is not verified.**
- The method is the one of `switch_bp` in Section 5, the algebraic branching equation of Allgower and Georg, *Numerical Continuation Methods* (Springer 1990), Section 8.3, eqs. (8.3.7) to (8.3.11). I read those equations for Section 5. I did not read a source that applies them to the collocation problem of a delay cycle. The step is mine: it treats the unknowns of Section 8l as a finite-dimensional system $F(x) = 0$ with $F: \mathbb{R}^{\dim} \to \mathbb{R}^{\dim - 1}$, which is what the corrector sees.
- The branch that results is a branch of the discretized problem. That it approximates a branch of cycles of the delay equation is the claim of Section 8q, and I did not prove it. The oracles below have branches that the discretization represents exactly, so they do not test that claim.
- The second derivative of $F$ is a central difference of the analytic Jacobian with a fixed step $10^{-5}$ along unit vectors. The step is my choice, near the cube root of the rounding unit. I did not study how the error of the roots depends on it.
- The branch that was followed is taken from the chord from the point before the `BPC` to the `BPC`. At a branch point with a small angle between the two branches the chord can be nearer to the wrong root. The tests have branches at 90 degrees (the pitchfork) and at about 84 degrees (the transcritical form, where the other branch has $\alpha$ and each of the $Nm + 1$ values of $z$ moving at the same rate), and nothing tests a small angle.
- The sign convention, the thresholds `ROOT_TOL` $= 10^{-6}$ and the singular-value ratios below are carried over from `switch_bp` or chosen by me, and none is derived. The sign tolerance is $10^{-6}$ here, where `switch_bp` uses $10^{-12}$, because the difference-quotient noise in a tangent component is about $10^{-8}$.
- A branch point where the form has a double root (a cusp of the branch) is refused, and so is a kernel of dimension 3 or more. The tests pin the first (test 9). A point where an even number of branches cross is not labeled `BPC` (Section 8q, test 8), so it is never offered to the switch.
- A branch from a run with `adapt` is refused. A profile on an adapted mesh has no tangent in the unknowns of a uniform mesh, and I did not write the move onto another mesh for this step.
- The switched branch starts at the `BPC` as the locator of Section 5 left it. The start is not corrected again: with $\alpha$ fixed the matrix is singular at a branch point, so the corrector of the start of Section 8n would fail there.
- The switch is not cancellable. The singular value decomposition of the $(\dim - 1) \times \dim$ Jacobian takes 13.6 s for $\dim = 2964$ on the development machine (values only, in a scratch test), and the vectors cost more. The function takes no `cancel` flag, and a run of the switched branch takes one.
- Delay cycles only. The ODE cycles of Section 7 have no `BPC`.

**Method.** Let $x_b = (\text{profile}, T, \alpha)$ be the point of the `BPC`, $J$ the Jacobian of Section 8l at $x_b$ with the phase reference set to the profile of $x_b$, and $\sigma_1 \ge \dots \ge \sigma_{\dim-1}$ its singular values. At a simple branch point $J$ has rank $\dim - 2$. The right kernel is spanned by an orthonormal pair $\varphi_1, \varphi_2$ (the last two columns of $V$), and the left kernel by one vector $\psi$ (the last column of $U$). With $c_{ij} = \psi^{T} F''(x_b)[\varphi_i, \varphi_j]$ a tangent $t = b_1\varphi_1 + b_2\varphi_2$ of a branch through $x_b$ satisfies

$$c_{11} b_1^2 + 2 c_{12} b_1 b_2 + c_{22} b_2^2 = 0.$$

The second derivative is $F''[\varphi_i, \varphi_j] \approx \bigl(J(x_b + h\varphi_j) - J(x_b - h\varphi_j)\bigr)\varphi_i / (2h)$ with $h = 10^{-5}$, and $c_{12}$ and $c_{21}$ are averaged. The phase row of $F$ is linear in $x$, so it adds nothing. The form is solved by `branching_root` of Section 5, which now takes the sign tolerance as an argument. Of its two root directions the one with the smaller $\lvert t^{T}v\rvert$ is taken, with $v$ the chord from the point before the `BPC` (`points[index - 1]`) to the `BPC`, normalized, in the unknowns $(\text{profile}, T, \alpha)$. The sign of $t$ is positive in $\alpha$ when $\lvert t_\alpha\rvert \ge 10^{-6}$, and otherwise positive in the first component with modulus at least $10^{-6}$. This is the convention of `switch_bp` with the tolerance changed. `branching_root` refuses with `BadProblem` when the form has no pair of real roots, has a double root, or has a non-finite coefficient.

The kernel check is that of `switch_bp`. The point is refused with `BPC is not simple` when $\sigma_{\dim-1} > 10^{-6}\sigma_1$ (no second null direction) or $\sigma_{\dim-2} \le 10^{-8}\sigma_1$ (a kernel of dimension 3 or more). One SVD gives the singular values, $V$ and $U$. `pc-linalg` gets a function that returns them together, since the existing `singular_values`, `null_right` and `null_left` each run their own SVD. A form that is zero up to difference-quotient noise is refused with the same message, because `branching_root` only refuses a form that is exactly zero: the point is refused when $\max_{ij}\lvert c_{ij}\rvert \le 10^{-8}\sigma_1$ or when that maximum is not a number. The central difference carries noise of about $2 \cdot 10^{-11}\sigma_1$ in $c$ (the diagonal entries of the pitchfork form are $5 \cdot 10^{-9}$ against $\sigma_1 = 297$), so the floor is 500 times the noise. The factor $10^{-8}$ is my choice, and the floor compares $c$ with $\sigma_1$, which has other units, so a model whose second derivatives are small against its first derivatives in the unknowns can be refused when its form is not zero.

**The API.** In `pc_engine::dde`, re-exported with the `dde_` prefix:

- `DdeCycleSwitch { start: DdeCycleStart, alpha: f64, tangent: Vec<f64> }`. `start` is the profile and period of the `BPC`, `alpha` its parameter value, and `tangent` the unit tangent of the other branch in the unknowns $(\text{profile}, T, \alpha)$, so its length is `n * (ntst * ncol + 1) + 2`.
- `switch_cycle_bpc(model, setup, branch: &DdeCycleBranch, special: usize) -> Result<DdeCycleSwitch, EngineError>`. `special` indexes `branch.specials`. Every error is `BadProblem`: `no special point {k}` when `k` is out of range; `special point {k} is a {label}, not a BPC` for another label; `the BPC is the first point of the branch; switching needs the point before it` when its `index` is 0 or larger than `points.len()`; `the branch has an adapted mesh` when the special or the point before it has a non-empty `mesh`; `the BPC does not fit the model` when a profile has the wrong length or any value is not finite (`check_setup` runs first); `the BPC coincides with the point before it` when the chord has length at most $10^{-12}(1 + \lVert x_b\rVert_2)$; and `BPC is not simple` as above. The checks run in this order.
- `continue_cycle_switch(model, setup, sw: &DdeCycleSwitch, opts, floquet: Option<&DdeFloquetOptions>, bpc: bool, cancel) -> Result<DdeCycleBranch, EngineError>`. It runs `run_branch` from the point of `sw` with `tangent0` set to `sw.tangent` and `start_is_special` set. The first point of the result is that point. The direction of the run is `opts.direction`: $+1$ follows `sw.tangent` and $-1$ follows its negative. The checks are those of `continue_cycle_on` in the same order, with a `tangent` of the wrong length or with a non-finite value giving `the switch does not fit the setup` after the step controls, and the start is not corrected. `adapt` has no argument. The `BPC` of the start is not reported again, and `bpc: true` finds later branch points on the new branch.

The check code of `continue_cycle_on` moves into one helper that `continue_cycle_switch` calls too, so the two cannot differ. `branching_root` becomes `pub(crate)`, and `switch_bp` passes $10^{-12}$ so that its results do not change.

**Command line.** `pc-cli dde-cycle FILE` with `branch: true` accepts the key `switch`, an object with two keys, both required: `special`, a non-negative integer, and `direction`, which is 1 or -1. With `switch` the command does two runs. The first is `continue_cycle_bpc` with the problem's `options` (no `floquet`, no `adapt`), and `special` indexes its specials. The second is `continue_cycle_switch` with the problem's `options` except that `direction` is the one of `switch`, with `floquet` and `bpc` as given in the problem (`bpc` absent means off). The output is the branch JSON of the second run with one more key, `switch`, an object with `special`, `alpha` and `period` (of the `BPC`). `switch` that is not an object, a missing or unknown key in it, a `special` that is not a non-negative integer, a `direction` other than 1 and -1, `switch` without `branch`, and `switch` with `adapt` are exit 2 naming `switch`. Errors of `switch_cycle_bpc` are exit 2 with the engine message. Without `switch` the output is that of Sections 8n to 8q.

**Acceptance tests.** Rust tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_switch.rs`, unit tests in `crates/pc-linalg/src/null.rs` and `crates/pc-engine/src/switch.rs`, and CLI tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`. $N = 20$, $m = 4$, `Evaluator::Jit` and `hmax` $= 0.1$ unless a test says otherwise. The models ZBLK (with its variants) and the helpers are those of Section 8q. In ZBLK a constant $z$ solves the $z$ collocation exactly, so the branch $z^2 = \mu - m_0$ lies on the discretized problem exactly at every mesh point, and the $x$ block does not change along it. The first run is ZBLK from $\mu = 0.1$ with `bounds` $(0.1, 0.6)$ and `bpc`, and `switch_cycle_bpc` takes its only special. The bounds below are set before the code exists and are revised in the measured values after.

1. The pitchfork. The switch is at $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-12}$. Its tangent has norm 1 to $10^{-12}$, an $x$ part, a $T$ entry and an $\alpha$ entry of modulus at most $10^{-6}$, and every $z$ entry equal to $1/\sqrt{Nm + 1}$ to $10^{-6}$ (so the sign is positive in $z$). With $J$ the Jacobian at the start (phase reference at the start profile), $\lVert J t\rVert_\infty \le 10^{-8}\lVert J\rVert_\infty$.
2. The switched run, direction $+1$, `max_steps` $= 400$: it stops with `Bounds`, its first point is the `BPC` ($\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-12}$), and at every point the $z$ values of all mesh points agree to $10^{-9}$, $z > 0$ after the first point, $\alpha$ does not decrease, and $\lvert z^2 - (\alpha - 0.3)\rvert \le 10^{-9}$. The last point has $\alpha = 0.6$ to $10^{-12}$. The $x$ values satisfy $\lvert x - \sin 2\pi s_q\rvert \le 10^{-7}$ and $\lvert T - 2\pi\rvert \le 10^{-10}$ (the collocation error of the $x$ block at $N = 20$, $m = 4$ is $5.3 \cdot 10^{-8}$; the switch does not add to it). With direction $-1$ the same holds with $z < 0$ and the same $z^2$ relation.
3. The transcritical form. The $z$ equation is `z'=(mu-m0)*z-z^2`, the other branch is $z = \mu - m_0$ (exact on the mesh), and the angle between the branches in the unknowns is about 84 degrees. The tangent has $t_\alpha > 0$, and $\lvert t_\alpha - t_z\rvert \le 10^{-6}$ with $t_z$ the common $z$ entry. Direction $+1$ gives $z > 0$ and $\alpha$ rising, direction $-1$ gives $z < 0$ and $\alpha$ falling, and both have $\lvert z - (\alpha - 0.3)\rvert \le 10^{-9}$ at every point. The run of direction $-1$ stops with `Bounds` at $\alpha = 0.1$.
4. The first run's direction does not matter. ZBLK from $\mu = 0.6$ with `direction` $-1$ gives a switch whose `alpha` agrees with test 1 to $10^{-12}$ and whose tangent agrees to $10^{-6}$. The same for the off-grid start $\mu = 0.137$ with $h_{\max} = 0.31$ and with $h_{\max} = 0.083$.
5. The nonlinear coefficient. The $z$ equation is `z'=(exp(8*mu)-exp(8*m0))*z-z^3` as in Section 8q, test 4, with `bounds` $(0.1, 0.4)$ so that $z$ stays below 4. The switched run has $\lvert z^2 - (e^{8\alpha} - e^{8 m_0})\rvert \le 10^{-8}$ at every point with direction $+1$.
6. The size. $N = 100$, $m = 4$: the tests 1 and 2 hold. With the fallback evaluator (`Evaluator::Fallback`) at $N = 20$, the tangent agrees with the Jit tangent to $10^{-6}$ and the run has the same number of points to within 2.
7. No second label at the start. The switched run of test 2 with `bpc: true` has no special, in particular none at index 0, and with `floquet` ($\mathrm{nhist} = 20$, $\mathrm{count} = 4$) every point has 4 multipliers and no warning.
8. The model with a fold. The model of Section 8q, test 5 (`BPC`, `LPC`, `BPC` along the run): `switch_cycle_bpc` at special 0 and at special 2 both succeed with the switched run having $\lvert z^2 - (a - 0.2)\rvert \le 10^{-8}$ at every point, and at special 1 (an `LPC`) it is `BadProblem` with `special point 1 is a LPC, not a BPC`.
9. The refusals. Each is `BadProblem` with the message of the section: `no special point 7` on the branch of test 1; a branch whose special has `index` 0, built by hand from the branch of test 1; a branch from a run with `adapt` ($\mathrm{every} = 3$, Section 8q test 7); a special whose point has a profile of the wrong length (`the BPC does not fit the model`); a special whose `point` is the point before it (`the BPC coincides with the point before it`); a special built by hand at an ordinary point of the $\mu$-line (the Jacobian has rank $\dim - 1$, `BPC is not simple`); and the `BPC` of test 1 offered with the model whose $z$ equation is `z'=(mu-m0)^2*z-z^3` (the profile is a valid orbit of that model at $\mu = m_0$, the Jacobian has the same two-dimensional kernel, and the form is 0 because the only terms of $F''$ in the left kernel have a factor $\mu - m_0$ or $z$, so `branching_root` refuses with `BPC is not simple`). `continue_cycle_switch` with a `tangent` of the wrong length is `BadProblem` with `the switch does not fit the setup`, a bad step control gives the step-control message first, and a `cancel` flag set at the call is `Cancelled`.
10. The pieces. Unit tests: the new SVD function of `pc-linalg` returns the same singular values as `singular_values` and a kernel basis equal up to sign to `null_right` and `null_left` on a random $5 \times 6$ matrix of rank 4 with a fixed seed (the kernels agree through their projectors); `branching_root` with the sign tolerance $10^{-6}$ takes the $\alpha$ sign from a component of $10^{-8}$ differently from the one with $10^{-12}$ (one gives the first component's sign, one the $\alpha$ sign); and the tests of `switch_bp` in Section 5 pass, with the two unit tests of `branching_root` taking the new sign tolerance (set to $10^{-12}$ there).
11. The old paths do not change. The tests of Sections 8n to 8q pass unchanged, with no edit to a test or a bound. They include the checks of `continue_cycle_floquet`, `continue_cycle_adapt` and `continue_cycle_bpc`, so the shared check helper keeps their order of refusals.
12. `pc-cli dde-cycle` with ZBLK (`branch: true`, `bpc: true`, `bounds` as in test 1, `switch` $= \{\text{special}: 0, \text{direction}: 1\}$): the output has a `switch` object with $\lvert\alpha - 0.3\rvert \le 10^{-11}$ and $\lvert\text{period} - 2\pi\rvert \le 10^{-10}$, and every point has $\lvert z^2 - (\alpha - 0.3)\rvert \le 10^{-8}$. With `direction` $-1$ the $z$ values are negative. With `floquet` the points have multipliers. The errors of the section (a bad `special`, a bad `direction`, an unknown key, `switch` as an array, `switch` without `branch`, `switch` with `adapt`, a `special` that is out of range, a `special` that is not a `BPC`) are exit 2, the first six naming `switch`. A problem without `switch` gives the output of Sections 8n to 8q.

**Measured values** (first run, `Evaluator::Jit`, from `print_tables` in the test file, which prints them with `cargo test --test dde_cycle_switch print_tables -- --ignored --nocapture`).
- Test 1, ZBLK at $N = 20$, $m = 4$ ($\dim = 164$): $\alpha - m_0 = -1.6 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $T - 2\pi = 1.9 \cdot 10^{-11}$, $\lvert\lVert t\rVert - 1\rvert = 0$, largest $x$ entry $3.4 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $T$ entry $-4.6 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $\alpha$ entry $8.0 \cdot 10^{-12}$, largest $z$ entry error $8.7 \cdot 10^{-16}$, $\lVert J t\rVert_\infty / \lVert J\rVert_\infty = 1.2 \cdot 10^{-16}$.
- Test 2, 57 points for either direction, stop `Bounds`: $z$ spread $1.3 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $\lvert z^2 - (\alpha - m_0)\rvert \le 2.4 \cdot 10^{-15}$, $x$ error $5.29 \cdot 10^{-8}$, $T$ error $1.9 \cdot 10^{-11}$, last $\alpha - 0.6 = -2.0 \cdot 10^{-14}$. The $x$ error is the collocation error of the first run's orbit, which the switch carries along unchanged: it is why the bound is $10^{-7}$ and not $10^{-8}$. I first wrote $10^{-8}$ without a measurement, and the test showed $5.3 \cdot 10^{-8}$.
- Test 3: $t_\alpha = 0.110$, $\max\lvert t_z - t_\alpha\rvert = 1.9 \cdot 10^{-11}$, $\lvert z - (\alpha - m_0)\rvert \le 2.5 \cdot 10^{-15}$, 34 points up and 25 down.
- Test 4: the `alpha` differences are at most $1.6 \cdot 10^{-15}$ and the tangent differences at most $1.1 \cdot 10^{-11}$.
- Test 5: 338 points, $\lvert z^2 - (e^{8\alpha} - e^{8 m_0})\rvert \le 8.9 \cdot 10^{-15}$.
- Test 6, $N = 100$: $\lVert J t\rVert / \lVert J\rVert = 1.5 \cdot 10^{-16}$. The fallback evaluator gives a tangent that equals the Jit tangent exactly and 57 points against 57.
- Test 8: $\alpha - 0.2 = -1.3 \cdot 10^{-15}$ at special 0 and $2.1 \cdot 10^{-15}$ at special 2, with at most $2.6 \cdot 10^{-15}$ in the $z$ relation.
- The form of the double-root model (test 9) has $\max\lvert c_{ij}\rvert = 1.1 \cdot 10^{-15}$ against $\sigma_1 = 297$, which is $4 \cdot 10^{-18}\sigma_1$. The smallest genuine form among the tests, that of $N = 100$, is $0.31$ against $\sigma_1 = 1485$, which is $2 \cdot 10^{-4}\sigma_1$. The floor lies between them.

## 8s. MATLAB front end for delay equations (phase P34)

Phase P34 puts the delay-equation engine of Sections 8c to 8r behind the same front door as the ODE engine: `phasecont.Model`, `phasecont.continuation`, `Branch` and `Session`. It is built in slices. Slice 1 is the one stated here: load a model with `delay(...)` calls and continue an equilibrium of it in one parameter, with the labels `H`, `LP` and `BP` of Section 8e. Each later slice (the Hopf curve of Section 8f, the coefficients of Sections 8g to 8k, and the cycles of Sections 8l to 8r) amends this section when it starts, and until it does the pages named above say "no MATLAB front end" for it.

Owner approval: the owner approved public API changes for this work on 2026-11-08 and delegated the choices below on the same date. The C ABI keeps its function signatures and `abi_version` stays 1, so route A (below) needs no further sign-off. A change to a signature would.

**Route A.** A delay model goes through the existing entries `model_from_ode`, `model_compile`, `run_start`, `run_poll` and `run_copy`. The ABI carries the model as an opaque id and the problem as JSON, so the new behavior is a new `Kind`, new JSON keys and a flag in `model_info`. Route B, new entries such as `dde_run_start`, would change the C ABI and is not used.

**Loading.** `model_from_ode` calls `pc_ir::lower` on the parsed text. When that fails, it calls `pc_ir::lower_dde`. `lower_dde` refuses a model with no `delay(...)` call, so one text cannot lower both ways. If `lower_dde` succeeds, the handle is a delay model. If both fail, the error is the one from `lower_dde` when the text has a `delay` call and the one from `lower` otherwise. A model that `lower` accepts is loaded as before, to the last bit.
- The handle has a flag `delay`, and `model_info` reports it as the new key `"delay"` (`true` or `false`).
- `variables`, `parameters`, `values` and `init` of `model_info` for a delay model list the state names, the user parameter names, their values and the declared start, with the user parameters only. The hidden delay slots of Section 8d are not listed and not counted by any buffer length. A `free` name is looked up among the user parameters only.

**Entries that refuse a delay model.** A delay handle is an error, with the text "the model has delay terms, and this entry is for ODE models", in `model_rhs`, `model_rhs_t`, `model_equilibrium`, `ens_start`, the `emit_c` option of `model_compile` and every run `type` except `"EP"`. `model_compile` with the JIT builds the model with `build_dde`, which checks that it lowers and compiles, and records the evaluator. `model_compile` with AOT is an error, since the code generators refuse delays (Section 8d), and the message is that of `build_dde`. `run_start` on a delay handle that was not compiled is the same error as for an ODE handle.

**The run.** The problem JSON is
```json
{"type": "EP", "free": "r", "roots": {"n": 30, "re_min": -0.5},
 "options": {"h0": 0.01, "hmax": 0.05, "max_steps": 200, "bounds": [1.2, 2.0]}}
```
- `roots` is required for a delay model, with `n` an integer of at least 1 and `re_min` a number below 0, the `RootOptions` of Section 8c. There is no default for either, so a run never counts roots with a setting the caller did not choose. An ODE model with a `roots` key is an error.
- `options` takes the keys that `dde-branch` takes (Section 8e). `options.detect.h` and `options.mesh_adapt` are errors, as there. `switch` and `start_is_special` are errors.
- `u0` has `n` values and `params` has the user parameters, one per name in `model_info`. A buffer of any other length is an error.
- The engine runs `dde::continue_equilibrium` once, on the worker thread, with the cancel flag of the run. The call returns the whole branch, so `run_poll` reports the state `running` with no points until the call ends, and then all points and specials at once. A run that is cancelled ends in the state `cancelled` and keeps no points, since `continue_equilibrium` returns `Cancelled` and no partial branch. The help text of `continuation` says this.
- `Kind::DdeEp` has its own row layout and no test values. A row is `[alpha, u(1..n), count]`, `n + 2` values, with `count` the number of roots with $\operatorname{Re}\lambda > 0$ (the $N$ of Section 8e) stored as a double. A row has no `h` and no step count, since the delay code does not report them. In the engine the count lives in `Point.tests[0]` as a float, with `tests[1]` and `tests[2]` NaN, so `Point` gains no field, and `pack_row` copies it to the last column. A special has the same row, `index` is `between[1]` of Section 8e, the label is `H`, `LP` or `BP`, `Extras.omega` is the $\omega$ of an `H` point, and `Extras.values` holds the pairs `unstable_before` and `unstable_after`. `Extras.refined` is false, since no special of this slice is refined by Newton.
- The first branch of a run is the placeholder that the ODE kinds also push, so that branch numbers match.

**MATLAB.**
- `phasecont.Model` reads `delay` from `model_info` and has the property `IsDelay`. `Model.Parameters` and the parameter vectors hold user parameters only.
- `phasecont.continuation(m, "EP", Free=name, Roots=struct(N=30, ReMin=-0.5), ...)` runs a delay model. `Roots` is required for a delay model and an error for an ODE model, with no default. `Type` other than `"EP"` is an error for a delay model. The options `MeshAdapt` and `Switch` are errors for a delay model, and so is `Detect` with `H`. `Detect` defaults to `["LP" "BP"]` for a delay model and `H` is reported from the root count whatever `Detect` says (Section 8e), so the default leaves out no label that the engine can report. A start point whose label is not `"EP"` is an error, since a delay run starts from an equilibrium.
- A delay branch is a `Branch` of type `"EP"` with `Eigenvalues` empty (0 columns), the new property `RootCount` (one value per point, empty for an ODE branch) and `Stable` equal to `RootCount == 0`. Its special points carry the label, the parameter value and the state, with an empty `Eigenvalues`, and for `H` the frequency in `Data.omega`. `Data.values` holds `unstable_before` and `unstable_after`. The `Tests` of the branch have no columns, since the delay code has no test functions.
- `Session` saves and loads a delay branch. The record keeps `Type = "EP"`, and `rows2branch` reads the delay row layout from `Model.IsDelay` of the model it is given, which the loaded session rebuilds from the stored source text. No field of the record or of the session file changes, so a session that an earlier release wrote loads as before.
- A delay model always uses the JIT evaluator. With the evaluator `"aot"` (the `Evaluator` option or the `Codegen` session option) a delay model is built with the JIT and no warning, since the code generators refuse delays (Section 8d) and the AOT route has no use for them.
- `Model.Diagnostics` keeps warning ODE0054 for a delay model, but `Model` does not raise it as a MATLAB warning, since the model runs through `phasecont.continuation`. The text of ODE0054 names the `pc-cli` commands only. It is updated at the end of P34, once the MATLAB entries exist that it should name.

**What this slice does not do.** It does not continue a Hopf point in two parameters, compute a Lyapunov coefficient, follow cycles, or run an initial value problem of the delay equation. Points arrive when the run ends and not one by one.

**Acceptance tests (slice 1).** Every model is our own.
1. Rust, `pc-ffi`: `model_from_ode` and `model_compile` accept the Hutchinson model `x' = r*x*(1-delay(x,tau))` with `par r=1.3, tau=1`. `model_info` reports `"delay": true`, `variables` `["x"]`, `parameters` `["r", "tau"]`, `values` `[1.3, 1]` and `n_p` 2. An ODE model reports `"delay": false` and its info is unchanged. A model whose `delay` call has the wrong form fails with a `Model` error that names the call. A model with a delay call and a syntax error reports the syntax error.
2. Rust, `pc-ffi`: on the delay handle, `model_rhs`, `model_rhs_t`, `model_equilibrium`, `model_compile` with AOT or with `emit_c`, `ens_start`, and `run_start` with each of `"LP"`, `"H"`, `"LC"`, `"LPC"`, `"PD"`, `"NS"`, `"HOM"` and `"IVP"` are errors with the text above and do not panic. A run of `"EP"` on an ODE handle with a `roots` key is an error.
3. Rust, `pc-ffi`: the Hutchinson run of Section 8e test 1, started through `run_start`, `run_poll` and `run_copy` with the same `n`, `re_min` and options, returns the same points and the same single special as `dde::continue_equilibrium` called directly: equal `alpha`, `x` and `count` at every point, an `H` at $r = \pi/2$ with $\omega = \pi/2$ within $10^{-9}$, `unstable_before` 0 and `unstable_after` 2, and the state `done` with the stop `Bounds`.
4. Rust, `pc-ffi`: a delay run with no `roots`, with `roots.n` of 0, with `roots.re_min` of 0 or above, with an unknown key in `roots`, with `detect.h`, with `mesh_adapt`, with `switch`, with `start_is_special`, with `params` of the wrong length, and with a hidden slot name as `free` is an error, and the message names the key. A run cancelled right after the start ends in the state `cancelled` with no points.
5. MATLAB, `tests/DelayBranchTest.m`: Hutchinson (Section 8e test 1), the delayed fold (test 4) and the delayed pitchfork (test 5) through `phasecont.continuation`, with the numbers of those tests within the tolerances given there. `Stable` and `RootCount` agree, `Eigenvalues` has no columns, `Roots` is required and refused on an ODE model, and a delay branch survives `Session` save and load.
6. `cargo test` and the ODE MATLAB tests stay green, and a session saved by the current release before P34 loads as before.

### Slice 2: the Hopf curve of a delay equilibrium

Slice 2 continues the Hopf curve of Section 8f in two parameters through the same run entries, with the options of Sections 8g-2, 8h and 8k. It is route A again: a new `Kind`, new JSON keys, no change to a function signature, and `abi_version` stays 1. It does not compute the coefficients at a `ZH` or `HH` point (Sections 8i and 8j), does not follow cycles, and does not start a curve from a `phasecont.Point` that is not an `H` point. Like slice 1 it returns the whole curve when the engine call ends.

**The run.** The problem JSON for a delay model is
```json
{"type": "H", "free": ["r", "tau"], "omega": 1.57, "lyapunov": false,
 "roots": {"n": 30, "re_min": -3.0},
 "options": {"hmax": 0.05, "max_steps": 400, "bounds": [0.5, 3.0]}}
```
- `type` `"H"` on a delay model is `Kind::DdeHopf`. On an ODE model it is the Hopf curve of Section 8 and takes none of the new keys. The refusal "a delay equation takes only \"EP\"" now allows `"H"` as well, and every other `type` still gets it.
- `free` is an array of two distinct user parameter names, with `bounds` on the second, as in Section 8f. The state `u0` (`n` values) and the parameters (one per user parameter) are the buffers of `run_start`, as for every kind, and the ABI refuses them as JSON keys. A buffer of any other length is an error, as in slice 1.
- `omega` is required, a number. It is the `omega` of `HopfSetup`. The parser refuses a value that is not above $10^{-8}$ (`DDE_HOPF_OMEGA_MIN`, the engine's own floor) with the text `omega: must be above 1e-8`, so a bad value never starts a run. The engine checks the value again, polishes the start (Section 8f), and a bad value there is `BadProblem`. An ODE model with an `omega` key is an error. There is no default, since a start without a frequency has no meaning.
- `lyapunov` is `true` or `false`, and `false` when the key is left out. With `true` the run computes $\ell_1$ at every point with $\kappa > 0$ and labels `GH` (Sections 8g-2 and 8h). Any other type is an error. An ODE model with a `lyapunov` key is an error.
- `roots` is optional here and required for `"EP"` on a delay model (slice 1). With it the run computes the roots at every point with $\kappa > 0$ and labels `HH` (Section 8k), with `n` and `re_min` read as in slice 1. Without it, `HH` is not looked for. The engine's start check of Section 8k applies: a start without exactly one critical root is `BadProblem`.
- `options` takes `h0`, `hmin`, `hmax`, `max_steps`, `tol_x`, `tol_f`, `max_newton`, `direction` and `bounds`. `detect` and `mesh_adapt` are errors, as for `dde-hopf`, and so are `switch` and `start_is_special`.
- A delay time as the second free parameter needs a positive lower bound in `bounds`, and the engine's `BadProblem` is passed on as it is.
- The engine runs `dde::continue_hopf_with` once on the worker thread with the cancel flag of the run. A cancelled run ends in the state `cancelled` with no points, as in slice 1. The first branch is the placeholder.

**Rows.** `Kind::DdeHopf` has `name` `"H"`, `labels` `["GH", "HH"]` and the stride $n + 5$. A row is `[alpha2, x(1..n), alpha1, kappa, l1, hh]`: the second free parameter first, as for the ODE Hopf curve, then the equilibrium, the first free parameter, $\kappa$, $\ell_1$ and $h$. `l1` and `hh` are NaN where the engine gives `None`, and always NaN without the option. In `Point`, `u` is `[x, alpha1, kappa]`, `alpha` is `alpha2`, `codim2` is `[l1, hh]` (always two entries, NaN where off), and `tangent`, `eigenvalues` and `mesh` are empty. `codim2_labels` is `["GH", "HH"]`, the names of the two test columns, and the labels of a special are those of Section 8f: `BT` and `ZH` always, `GH` with `lyapunov` and `HH` with `roots`.

A special has the row of its point with `l1` and `hh` NaN. `index` is the `index` of the engine special. `Extras.omega` is $\sqrt\kappa$ for $\kappa > 0$ and none otherwise. `Extras.coefficient` is `l2` of a `GH` special, and `Extras.normalization` for `GH` is the text `|q|=1, p Delta'(i omega) q=1`, which is the normalization of Section 8g-2 ($\|q\|_2 = 1$ and $p\,\Delta'(i\omega)\,q = 1$ with $p$ not conjugated, the constant `Q_NORM`). It is a delay-specific text and not the `GH_NORMALIZATION` of the ODE curve, which has another second condition. The other specials have no normalization. `Extras.values` holds `omega2` for an `HH` special where the engine has it. `Extras.refined` is false.

**MATLAB.**
